The donut's legend shows every value; the data table is the screen-reader path, and only with `tableView="details"` a path for sighted keyboard users (whether BarChart defaults to it is open). The bar tooltip does not meet WCAG 1.4.13 yet (no Escape, not hoverable): the island's column mode would close it. (Week 11: BarChart now defaults to the island's column mode and the gap is closed under it; below.)
  - **d3:** only the curve factories of `d3-shape` are bundled (`_core/path.ts` drives them through a string context), and the donut's paths are hand-written ([D-av](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)); `d3-array` gives `nice` and `ticks`.
  - **The island** is about 0.8 KB (813 B own code against §17.2's 0.8 KiB; 1,080 B with ChartTooltip and `cx`, what a page downloads, gated separately at 1.2 KiB for now), `role="application"`, and its server-built JSON is about 335 B per point ([D-ay](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)): bucket a daily series weekly above about 90 points. No `style` attribute is in any server HTML; the island positions through CSSOM after hydration, by `translate` only. Leaving the layer empties its live region, so returning speaks the point again. A pointer-shown card hides with Escape only while the layer has focus (WCAG 1.4.13 wants it without focus: about 70 B, over the island's headroom; open). (Closed in week 11, with the new island figure and payload; below.)
  - **Scales:** no chart takes a scale prop; `ScaleLike` and the exported `linear`, `band` and `point` serve custom renderers inside `ChartFrame` ([D-bd](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)).
  - **`ChartFrameProps`** grew past the sketch: `pending`, `tableView` (`'hidden'` default, `'details'`; [D-aw](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)), `ticks` (`x`, `xAlign: 'point' | 'band'`, `y`), `xDirection`, `legend`, `empty`, `error`, `labels`, and `render` on the `<figure>`; `chart-frame.contract.ts` is the source.
  - **Reveal:** the clip-path sweep ends outset by 0.75rem (`inset(-0.75rem)`), so end dots and the outer half of a stroke are not cut.
  - **`xDirection="auto"`** mirrors the x labels but not the plot yet (the island would need the resolved direction); use `'rtl'`. Open. (Closed in week 11: it mirrors the plot too; below.)
  - **Forced colours:** system colours for every mark and legend swatch ([D-ax](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)).
- **As built in week 11 (2026-10-20, M40; where it differs from the week 10 bullets above, this wins; [§21.2](#212-v02--dashboard-shell-and-overlays--weeks-914) "Week 11 status", `packages/charts/W11-RESULTS.md`):**
  - **One API shape** (`src/_core/api.ts`): `Accessor<T, V>`, a key of the datum or `(datum, index)`, for AreaChart's and BarChart's `x`/`y` (and AreaChart's `compare`), DonutChart's `category`/`value` and Sparkline's `y`; `format` is `Intl.NumberFormatOptions` only, default `{}` (Intl's own), with `axisFormat` defaulting to its compact form; `xTicks: { every?, max? }` and `yTicks` on both cartesian charts; `XLabels extends ChartFrameLabels`; each chart's props include the figure attributes.
  - **BarChart** defaults to `interaction="nizivo"`: the island's **column mode** (one tab stop; arrow keys, Home and End over the columns; `data-highlighted` on the active band; the ChartTooltip card beside the bar; the polite region speaks title, series and value). Hover goes through the island and does not speak. `interaction="none"` keeps the per-band CSS tooltip (zero JS, hover only, short of WCAG 1.4.13). DonutChart stays `interaction="none"`: `tableView="details"` is its sighted keyboard route, and its keyboard is **open**.
  - **The island** is 1,457 B with ChartTooltip and `cx` (§17.2 figure 1.6 KiB on that graph; its own code is 1,217 B and has no entry of its own). Escape hides a pointer-shown card wherever focus is, and Escape on the focused plot is the card's alone, so an enclosing dialog stays open (WCAG 1.4.13: dismissable; persistent, no timeout; the card takes no pointer events and stays while the pointer is over the plot, card included, though it follows the band under the pointer, so it is not "hoverable"). Hiding the card empties the live region, so the next key speaks again. A new `xDirection` remounts the island (AreaChart and BarChart key it by the prop), which reads the plot's direction once. Under forced colours the card takes the system palette (`forced-color-adjust: auto`), the crosshair and dots keep theirs. Its props are compact: `series` once, the delta label once, the list `separators` once, points as `[title, values, y, change?, trend?]` tuples, no x and no `announce` (joined on the client), about 64 B of JSON a point. A daily series above about 90 points is still best bucketed weekly, for legibility (2 px a point on a 720 px plot at 365) and keyboard steps; bytes no longer force it (13.9 KB gzip of HTML, RSC payload and island together at 90 points).
  - **Paths** are at 2 decimals (`PATH_DIGITS`, [D-av](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)).
  - **`xDirection="auto"`** mirrors the plot, its marks and the x labels through CSS `:dir(rtl)`, in the server HTML before any script runs; the island reads the plot's computed direction once after hydration. It follows the `dir` attribute, not the CSS `direction` property, and a runtime `dir` change needs a remount; an engine without `:dir()` drops only `'auto'`'s mirroring.
  - **ChartFrame** renders an x track with no label as an empty cell in point mode (less HTML on long series).
  - **Exports:** `./area-chart` joins the map (§17.2: 7.1 KiB). `nz api` records every charts entry in `contracts/api/charts/**`, and the docs charts pages are generated routes that render their tables from it.
  - **Open:** DonutChart's keyboard; `role="application"` until the manual screen-reader pass (D-ay); forced colours for the accessibility owner (D-ax); real Safari, the Safari/iOS 17.5 floor and real touch hardware.
- **As built in week 12 (2026-10-20, M41 item 2; where it differs from the bullets above, this wins; [§21.2](#212-v02--dashboard-shell-and-overlays--weeks-914) "Week 12 status", `packages/react/W12-RESULTS.md`):**
  - **DonutChart** defaults to `interaction="nizivo"`: the island's **item mode** (column mode plus server-placed slice centres): one tab stop with a round focus ring, ArrowLeft/ArrowRight over the slices in data order (clockwise, never mirrored, since the ring has no `xDirection`), Home and End; keys start at the first slice, or at the last one a key or the pointer showed (week 12 review A11Y-W12-6); `data-highlighted` on the active segment dims the others and swaps the centre to its share and name; the ChartTooltip card sits beside the slice centre, toward the legend, with the slice's swatch and the share in its delta slot ("52% of total"); the polite region speaks the strings formatted on the server. The pointer picks a slice by element; the hole and the gaps keep the last card. Escape is as in the other modes (M41 item 1). Every slice renders a segment (`d=""` for a sliver thinner than the gap), so a sliver is reachable by keyboard. `interaction="none"` keeps the zero-JS CSS ring. The hidden table stays the screen-reader path; `tableView="details"` is no longer DonutChart's sighted keyboard route ([D-aw](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)). Under forced colours the active segment takes a 3 px CanvasText outline ([D-ax](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)). Figma 03 §4.6 draws no tooltip for the donut ("the centre label is the tooltip"): the card is a deliberate M41 deviation, kept alongside the centre swap.
  - **Sizes:** the island's client graph 1,569 B (figure **1.7 KiB**: 1,457 + 10 items + 20 the server-placed centre + 71 the element hit test + 11 the slice swatch), 1,580 B after the week 12 review's Escape fix (+11); `./donut-chart` 4,845 B with the island graph inside (figure **5.3 KiB**; 3,162 B in week 11), 4,856 B after the review. Both are labelled §17.2 raises (M41 item 2).
  - **Escape on the plot, fixed in the week 12 review (A11Y-W12-1):** the document listener's `preventDefault` kept a native `<dialog>` open but not `@nizivo/react`'s Dialog, whose React Aria wrapper handles Escape in a React `onKeyDown` on the popup before the document listener runs, and closed the whole dialog. The layer's own `onKeyDown` now takes Escape while the card shows (hides it, `stopPropagation`, `preventDefault`) in all three modes; with focus elsewhere the document listener still hides a pointer-shown card without consuming the key (M41 item 1). The donut spec checks it with a dialog that listens in React, and a probe confirmed it with the real Dialog in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox.
  - **Open:** `role="application"` until the manual screen-reader pass (D-ay); forced colours for the accessibility owner (D-ax); real Safari, the Safari/iOS 17.5 floor and real touch hardware.
- **As built after M42 (2026-10-20; [§23.3](#233-maintainer-decisions-2026-10-14) M42 items 6 and 15; `packages/react/W12-RESULTS.md` "After M42"):**
  - **`titleVisibility`** (`'visible'` default, `'hidden'`; type `ChartTitleVisibility`, exported from `./chart-frame` and the barrel) is a `ChartFrameProps` member, so AreaChart, BarChart and DonutChart take it identically through `ChartFramePassthrough` (M40's one API shape; the conformance suite runs every status with it hidden and fails a part that drops it). `'hidden'` adds the existing `nz-chart-visually-hidden` class to the `<figcaption>`: clipped to 1 × 1 and out of flow, never `display: none`, so the title and summary stay in the server HTML, the figure keeps its accessible name and the title still labels the data table. No new class, `data-*` attribute or hook. DonutChart's grid moves the ring and legend into the first row through `:has()`. Sparkline has no frame. The Next fixture's 08 TrendCard uses it, as Figma's Revenue Card draws no chart caption.
  - **The surface behind a chart:** the three parts that paint "the surface behind them" read the shared `--nz-surface` ([§8.3](#83-the-state-vocabulary-one-list-engine-independent)) as an optional middle fallback: `var(--nz-sparkline-halo, var(--nz-surface, var(--nz-bg-surface)))`, and the same for `--nz-area-chart-halo` and `--nz-chart-tooltip-halo` (the island's dot ring). A part's own hook still wins; with no Card on the page (no `@nizivo/react` at all) the halo is `bg/surface` as before. Inside a tinted KpiTile or TrendCard the ring takes the tint, a deviation from Figma 04, which draws it in `bg/surface` (figma/04 lists that white ring as a defect). Left alone on purpose: the donut's gaps are real gaps (the page shows through), and ChartTooltip's card and BarChart's CSS tooltip paint `bg/surface-raised` as their own surface. No charts baseline moved (the charts visual pages set no `--nz-surface`).
  - **Sizes after M42** (gzip, size-limit): `./chart-frame` 1,820 B, `./area-chart` 6,711, `./bar-chart` 5,407, `./donut-chart` 4,885, the barrel 9,296 (limit 9.7 KiB, unchanged), `styles.css` 3,506 (limit 3.7 KiB, unchanged); every charts limit is unchanged by M42 (the M42 review, F1); the island's client graph 1,580 B, unchanged. Measured + 10 % would pass the figure for `./area-chart` and `{ AreaChart }` from the barrel (7.3 against 7.1) and `./bar-chart` (5.9 against 5.7), so those limits stay at their figures: 559 B, 562 B and 429 B of headroom.
- **v0.3:** Funnel and Heatmap (HTML/CSS). Built in week 26 (D-de, D-df). Figma's heatmap ramp is chart/primary at five alphas (8 to 100 %), not solid sequential colours; solid steps for dark stay a design question.

### 14.2 Icons (`@nizivo/icons`)

- **Generated** by a ~150-line codegen plus SVGO 4 from **exact-pinned `lucide-static` 1.56.0**. One ESM module per icon, no directive (server-safe), `currentColor`, `sideEffects: false`. The full Lucide set is generated (unused icons cost nothing).
- **Week 4 (built; D-ad):** `packages/icons/scripts/generate.ts` writes 1,879 Lucide icons and the Instagram glyph (1,880 modules) at build time; the sources are not committed (`manifest.json`, one hash per icon plus the versions, is the reviewable record, and a test fails when a shipped name disappears with no alias). SVGO 4.1.0's preset is **not** lossless on Lucide (it changed pixels on 602 of 1,880 icons and mis-reflects a curve in `drama`), so the generator turns off two plugins (`mergePaths`, `convertShapeToPath`) and five `convertPathData` options; the pass then saves 2.35% of path characters, pixel-identical in a Chromium comparison of every icon. Deviations from the bullets below: the default `size` is `'1em'`, not 20 (the shipped slots size glyphs by `font-size`; a standalone 20 px icon passes `size={20}`); the accessible name is `label` (role `img` + `aria-label`, no `<title>`, so no hover tooltip), not `title`; `absoluteStrokeWidth` sets `vector-effect: non-scaling-stroke` (the formula below is NaN for `1em`); the notices are in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` (generated and checked by `test/generate.test.ts`), and `LICENSE` stays Apache-2.0; `aliases.json` starts empty (Lucide's 264 deprecated names are not shipped); brand glyphs are only at `@nizivo/icons/brands/<name>`, never in the barrel. The example code below is the original sketch: Lucide 1.56.0 draws `calendar` with `M8 2v3` and `y="3"`. The RTL flip is `:dir(rtl) [data-mirror='rtl'] { scale: -1 1 }` in `@nizivo/react/css/base.css`. Measured: one icon from the barrel is 502 B gzip in a minified consumer bundle, each further icon 71 B, ten icons 1,137 B (`packages/icons/test/bundle.test.ts`, rolldown through tsdown; `packages/react/W4-RESULTS.md`).
- **Stroke (settled 7):** default `size={20}` and **`strokeWidth={1.92}` on the 24 viewBox, non-absolute.** That renders 1.6 px at 20 px and 1.28 px at 16 px, exactly what Figma draws for 20 px icons and the 16 px Button icons (02). `absoluteStrokeWidth` is opt-in (use `strokeWidth={1.6}` with it for 1.6 px at every size). The stroke is an SVG attribute, never a CSS `calc()`: verdict V11 showed a `calc()` over a length variable collapses to 1 user unit, and CSS beats the attribute fallback.
- `aria-hidden` unless `title` is given (week 4: `label`, or a consumer `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`; D-ad).
- **`mirror.json`:** a curated directional subset gets `data-mirror="rtl"`. Clocks, checks, search, rings and trend icons never mirror.
- **`aliases.json`** keeps names stable across Lucide renames, or when regenerating from another source.
- **Brand glyphs** at `@nizivo/icons/brands/<name>`, from simple-icons (CC0), starting with Instagram plus its usage rule ("only for Connect / Follow on Instagram links").
- `LICENSE` carries the Lucide ISC and Feather MIT notices; CI checks they are present. The notice is needed wherever Lucide path data ships, so `packages/react` also ships a `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` file for the glyphs in `_internal/glyphs.tsx` (Lucide ISC; the Feather MIT notice is added if a Feather-derived glyph is inlined). W1: `packages/react/test/unit/notices.test.ts` checks that the file is in `files` and carries the ISC text; it runs in `pnpm test`. The check on `@nizivo/icons`' notices came with the generator in week 4: `packages/icons/test/generate.test.ts` checks that `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` equals the generated text and carries Lucide's ISC licence, the Feather MIT notice and the CC0 notice.
- **Core imports no icon package.** A lint bans `@nizivo/icons` and `lucide*` inside `packages/react`: oxlint's `no-restricted-imports` in `.oxlintrc.json` (week 4). dependency-cruiser is not set up. At most 10 internal glyphs are inlined from one list in `_internal/glyphs.tsx`: spinner, error mark, check, indeterminate dash, chevron (rotated for sort, select and pagination), chevrons-up-down, close, eye/eye-off, plus/minus, calendar. Each is overridable through a slot prop on the part that renders it, so an icon-set swap leaves no Lucide-stroke glyph behind:

  | Glyph | Part | Slot prop |
  |---|---|---|
  | spinner | Button, IconButton (`pending`) | `pendingIndicator` |
  | chevrons-up-down | `Select.Trigger` | `indicator` |
  | check, indeterminate dash | Checkbox | `indicator` |
  | error mark | `FieldError`, InlineError | `icon` |
  | close | `Dialog.Close`, Toast close, Alert dismiss | `render` on `Dialog.Close` (its `children` are text, week 6, D-an); `children` on `AlertDismiss` |
  | chevron | Table sort button; Pagination | `indicator`; `previousIcon` / `nextIcon` |
  | eye / eye-off | PasswordInput | `showIcon` / `hideIcon` |
  | plus / minus | QuantityStepper | `incrementIcon` / `decrementIcon` |
  | calendar | DateRangePicker trigger | `icon` |

  A conformance case per part renders a custom slot and asserts that the internal glyph is gone.

  **W2 state.** `_internal/glyphs.tsx` holds `spinner` and `chevrons-up-down`. The error mark is Lucide's `circle-x` (what Figma's Alert Critical draws) and close is Lucide's `x`. Both are on Lucide's list of Feather-derived icons, so inlining them also needs the Feather MIT notice in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` and in `test/unit/notices.test.ts` (today the file says neither inlined glyph is Feather-derived). Until they land, `InlineError`'s `icon` and `AlertDismiss`'s `children` are **required** props, and become optional with the glyph as default afterwards. **W3 state:** done. `glyphs.tsx` holds `spinner`, `chevrons-up-down`, `circle-x`, `close`, `check` and `minus` (Checkbox's mark and dash, also Feather-derived); `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` lists all six by their Lucide names and carries the Feather MIT notice, and `notices.test.ts` checks both. `InlineError`'s `icon` and `AlertDismiss`'s `children` are optional, with `circle-x` and `close` as defaults. Icons Figma draws that are not in this list stay consumer slots with no default: Alert's tone icons (info, circle-check, triangle-alert, circle-x) and Delta's trend icons (trending-up, trending-down, minus) go through each component's `icon` slot, filled from `@nizivo/icons`.
- **`IconProps`** (`size`, `strokeWidth`, `absoluteStrokeWidth`, `title`; week 4: `label` replaces `title`, and `mirror` opts a directional icon out of the flip) is the Lucide-shaped stroke contract of `@nizivo/icons`. Regenerating from another stroke icon set keeps it; a non-stroke source changes it, so it ships as a major or as a separate package.
- If icons slip from v0.1, docs fall back to `lucide-react` with `strokeWidth={1.92}` through the same slots.

```tsx
// packages/icons/src/calendar.tsx — GENERATED. No directive.
export function Calendar({ size = 20, strokeWidth = 1.92, absoluteStrokeWidth = false, title, ...rest }: IconProps) {
  const sw = absoluteStrokeWidth ? (Number(strokeWidth) * 24) / Number(size) : strokeWidth;
  return (
    <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" width={size} height={size} fill="none" stroke="currentColor" strokeWidth={sw}
         strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round" aria-hidden={title ? undefined : true} role={title ? 'img' : undefined} {...rest}>
      {title && <title>{title}</title>}
      <path d="M8 2v4" /><path d="M16 2v4" /><rect width="18" height="18" x="3" y="4" rx="2" /><path d="M3 10h18" />
    </svg>
  );
}
```

### 14.3 Media

- **`Image`** is a server-safe `<img>`: `width`, `height` and `alt` are required; `data-ratio` presets (`1/1`, `4/5`, `3/2`, `16/9`) map to `aspect-ratio` in CSS; **no inline `style`** (it would be blocked under a strict CSP and never repaired by hydration; gap report B §B1.1). `priority` maps to `fetchpriority="high"` with eager loading.
- `render={<NextImage … />}` (or unpic) swaps in a framework image component. Nizivo has no image provider.
- **No Figma raster ships anywhere** (packages, docs, Storybook, registry, fixtures, a Community copy) until it has an entry in `ASSETS.md`. The 3D renders, product photos and portraits have unknown provenance (G7).
- Instagram media goes through an app-side proxy or cache; components never fetch from or script Meta (gap report B §B7).

### 14.4 Fonts

- **The library ships no font files** (settled 15). Geist and Geist Mono are OFL-1.1 and must not be vendored under the code licence. The `geist` npm package peers on Next only.
- Font tokens reference a variable with fallbacks: `--nz-font-family-sans: var(--font-geist-sans, "Geist"), ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;`. Next apps set `--font-geist-sans` through `next/font`; other frameworks load Geist themselves or accept the system stack.
- Numeric text styles always set `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`.

### 14.5 Opt-in heavy packages (`@nizivo/lab`)

- **Not created before 1.0, and only when a consumer asks.** The Figma "3D" items are raster images, not 3D scenes. The Rays and Mesh shader sources could not be retrieved from the file (09). SVG-displacement liquid glass works only in Chromium.
- When it exists: independent 0.x versioning, peer `react ^19.3.0` (`use(browser())`), optional peers (`@paper-design/shaders-react`, three), lazy loading, and a server-rendered poster. Our own `ShaderSurface` measured 1.4 KB ([r6 §4](research/r6-heavy-modules.md)).
- **CSS glass is core:** `Surface material="glass"` (v0.3) uses `backdrop-filter` (Safari 18; below that an opaque fallback), with opaque fallbacks under `@supports not`, `forced-colors` and `prefers-reduced-transparency`. Built in week 25 (D-dc).

## 15. Accessibility baseline and token remediation

### 15.1 Baseline

| Area | Commitment |
|---|---|
| Standard | WCAG 2.2 AA. **Any AA failure in light mode blocks a stable release.** Dark is reported but not blocking while it is `preview`. *Changed 2026-10-25 by M62: dark left preview, and an AA failure in dark blocks too (the docs smoke's dark pass fails the run)* |
| Focus | 2 px solid outline, offset 2 px, in `nz.base` (forced-colours safe). `border/focus` measures 4.07–4.66:1 on light backgrounds (green/500) and 5.87–8.17:1 on dark (green/300). The halo is decoration. Invalid fields keep the green outline and switch only the halo to critical |
| Target size | Every pointer target ≥ 24 × 24 px (2.5.8). Controls are 32/40/48. On a coarse **primary** pointer, an invisible `::after` expands hit areas to 44 px on **both** axes (a 40 × 40 icon button grows 3 px per side; a 120 × 40 button grows only vertically). `data-nz-touch-targets="always"` opts hybrid devices in. Hit areas never change the visible size. **Bare triggers (M27, week 7):** a `Dialog.Trigger` or `Tooltip.Trigger` with no `render` renders its own unstyled `<button>`; it adds `nz-hit-target` and its component CSS gives it `min-inline-size` and `min-block-size: 1.5rem` (24 px), so a short label or a lone glyph meets 2.5.8 beside another target. A composed Button or IconButton is already 32 px or more; an element passed through `render` keeps its own size. The docs still compose a Button. **Exception, text fields under a coarse pointer (M25, week 7):** Input, Textarea, NativeSelect and the Select trigger use 16 px text (Body/L, Label/L) so iOS Safari does not zoom on focus. That text is the one visible change under a coarse pointer: Input, NativeSelect and Select keep 32/40/48 px, and a Textarea row grows to Body/L's 25.6 px line (from 21 px) |
| Type | Floor **12 px** (0.75rem), linted (decision 8c, 2026-10-14; this reverses the original 14 px floor from critique S5). 12 px is allowed only through the named `Body/S` and `Label/S` styles (`--nz-font-body-s`, `--nz-font-label-s`; names confirmed by the week 1 dry run), never as an ad-hoc size. Small text gets no contrast discount: every colour used at 12 px must reach 4.5:1, and the contrast matrix checks those pairs. Components keep the sizes Figma draws; moving one to 12 px (for example Field help or error text) is a one-line hook change, listed on the Deviations page. Sizes in rem so user scaling works |
| Colour | Never colour-only: Field errors carry an icon and text (Figma recolours only help text, which fails 1.4.1) |
| Forced colours | Overlay popups get `border: 1px solid CanvasText`; focus is an outline. Forced colours replace backgrounds and drop shadows, so every state painted with a fill or a shadow gets a system-colour rule generated from `stateIndicator` ([§8.5](#85-the-a11y-floor-nzbase)): the Switch track and thumb, the Checkbox and Radio checked state, the selected segment, progress and meter fills, and selected menu and select items. The selected segment also draws a 1 px `border/control` ring in both modes, because its thumb is 1.04:1 against the track. Two week 2 caveats ([§8.5](#85-the-a11y-floor-nzbase)): a component that paints the state with `background` repeats the rule in its own CSS (ProgressBar, Meter), and ProgressRing's stroke-painted arc has its rules only in `progress-ring.css` until a `stroke` paint exists. Forced-colours screenshots of every v0.1 control gate v0.1 (exit criterion 14) |
| Motion | Reduced-motion token swap ([§13](#13-motion)); loops stop (`--nz-duration-pulse` = 0 ms); the pending spinner stops too and Button shows a visible "Loading…" text (M34, 2026-10-18; it kept turning before) |
| Semantics | Native elements first; APG patterns through the engine; tables are `<table>`, never `role="grid"`; KPI tiles are named groups, not landmarks |
| AT testing | Manual matrix every minor (NVDA, JAWS, VoiceOver macOS/iOS, TalkBack, Narrator, forced colours, 200% zoom). Guidepup NVDA/VoiceOver nightly from v1.0, non-blocking until green for 30 days |

### 15.2 Failures in the Figma tokens today (all reproduced by gap report C §3.1)

|---|---|---|
| `text/tertiary` n500, light, on n0 / n25 / n50 / n100 | 4.31 / 4.16 / 4.01 / **3.76** | 4.5 |
| `text/tertiary` n500, dark, on n950 / n900 / n850 / n800 | 4.34 / 4.03 / 3.65 / **3.12** | 4.5 |
| dark `text/critical` red/500 on n900 / n850 / n800 | 4.01 / 3.63 / **3.11** | 4.5 |
| dark `text/info` blue/500 on n900 / n850 | 3.63 / 3.29 | 4.5 |
| dark `bg/critical` red/500 with white / n900 / n950 | 4.33 / 4.01 / 4.32 (no passing foreground) | 4.5 |
| `border/default` n200 / `border/strong` n300 vs n0 (input boundaries) | **1.27 / 1.57** | 3 |
| `icon/warning` amber/500 vs n0 / amber/50 | **2.45** / 2.27 | 3 |
| Focus halo alone (green/500 @ 28%) vs n0 | **1.45** | 3 (fixed by the outline) |
| dark Toast action lime/400 on white; dark success icon green/300 on white | 1.75; 2.29 | 4.5; 3 |

### 15.3 Token remediation (computed)

All pairs below were recomputed with the WCAG 2.2 relative-luminance formula against every background each token can sit on (gap report C §3.2), and all pass. The two tables follow the placement rule in [§7.6](#76-code-owned-tokens).

**Overrides:** existing Figma variables re-pointed, in `code-owned/a11y/{light,dark}.overrides.tokens.json`.

| Token | Mode | Was (worst) | Now | Worst ratio now | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `text/secondary` | L | n600 (5.65) | n700 `#414741` | **8.31** | 4.5 |
| `text/secondary` | D | n400 (5.32) | n300 `#cbd0c8` | **8.58** | 4.5 |
| `text/tertiary` | L | n500 (3.76) | n600 `#596058` | **5.65** | 4.5 |
| `text/tertiary` | D | n500 (3.12) | n400 `#9ea59c` | **5.32** | 4.5 |
| `text/critical` | D | red/500 (3.11) | red/400 `#e47962` (new primitive, a proposal) | **4.62** | 4.5 |
| `text/info` | D | blue/500 (2.81) | blue/400 `#7098e0` (new primitive, a proposal) | **4.65** | 4.5 |
| `bg/critical` | D | red/500: no passing foreground | red/600 (as in light) | **5.69** with white | 4.5 |
| `icon/warning`, `border/warning` | L | amber/500 (2.13) | amber/600 `#b07b0e` | **3.22** | 3 |
| `icon/critical` | D | red/500 (3.11, which already passes 3:1) | red/400 | **4.62** | 3 (a **pairing** override, not a contrast fix: it keeps the icon on the same red as `text/critical`) |

**Proposals:** new variables Figma does not have yet. Semantic colours and motion values are in `code-owned/proposals/{color,motion}/<mode>.tokens.json`, one file per mode; primitives and text styles are in `code-owned/proposals.tokens.json` ([§7.6](#76-code-owned-tokens)).

| Token | Mode | Was (worst) | Now | Worst ratio now | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `text/accent` | L / D | — (Toast action used `bg/accent`) | green/700 / lime/300 | **7.91** / **10.68** | 4.5 |
| `text/on-critical` | L+D | — | white on `bg/critical` | **5.69** | 4.5 |
| `bg/critical-hover` + white | L+D | raw red/700 bind | red/700 | **8.26** | 4.5 |
| `icon/info` | L / D | Alert used `icon/brand` | blue/600 / blue/400 | **5.83** / **4.65** | 3 |
| `border/control` (input, checkbox, radio, switch-off track, select trigger) | L / D | n200/n300 (1.27/1.57) | neutral/450 `#828a81` (new) / n500 | **3.10** / **3.12** | 3 |
| `border/control-hover` | L / D | n300 / n700 | n500 / n400 | **3.76** / **5.32** | 3 |
| `chart/warning` (interim alias of `icon/warning`) | L | `text/warning` misused as a fill | amber/600 | **3.22** on `chart/track` | 3 |
| `chart/critical` (interim alias of `chart/negative`) | L+D | `text/critical` misused as a fill | red/500 | **3.77** light / **3.10** dark, on `chart/track` | 3 |

**Dark surface and border changes that go with it** (overrides; gap report C §2.3): dark `bg/subtle` n900 → new n925 (the subtle band reappears at 1.03:1, matching light's 1.04); dark `border/subtle` n850 → n800, `border/default` n800 → n700, `border/strong` n700 → n600; every floating surface uses `bg/surface-raised` (n850). On n850, hover rows (n800) stay distinct at 1.17:1.

**New primitives** (proposals; OKLCH-interpolated between ramp neighbours, so hue and chroma follow the ramp): neutral/450 `#828a81`, neutral/925 `#151816`, red/400 `#e47962`, blue/400 `#7098e0`.

**The text ladder: Option A** (recommended, design question 8e). It re-spaces the ladder with existing primitives: light 900/700/600, dark 50/300/400. It keeps the designer's secondary-to-tertiary step (ΔL\* 10.5 light, 15.9 dark). Option B (new steps) would compress that step to about 1.2:1, so tertiary nearly merges with secondary. If design rejects A, use C (alias tertiary to secondary) rather than B. The cost of A: light secondary text gets one step deeper on about 20 components, so the render review must include **light** pages 02, 04, 05 and 08.

**Component remapping:** Input, Select trigger, Search Field, Checkbox and Radio boundaries use `border/control` (hover: `border/control-hover`). `border/default` and `border/subtle` become decorative only (cards, dividers). The Switch off-track fill uses `border/control`'s colour (4.31:1 light, 4.03:1 dark). Bare close icons (Toast 16 px, Alert 16 px, Banner 18 px, Modal 20 px) become `IconButton`s (Alert's is `AlertDismiss`: a 24 px button in week 2, a tertiary `sm` IconButton since week 8, M28; [§9.5](#95-per-component-tier-table-core)); Alert Info uses `icon/info`; Critical hover uses `bg/critical-hover` instead of the raw red/700 bind. ProgressBar's warning and critical fills use `chart/warning` and `chart/critical`. The selected SegmentedControl segment draws `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--nz-border-control)` plus the weight change, in both modes (the thumb is 1.04:1 against the track; handoff §4.9).

**Week 2 corrections to the remapping** (from the contrast matrix on the built `tokens.css`, `packages/tokens/CONTRAST.md`):
- **Progress fills are measured on `chart/track`, the background they are painted on.** The `chart/warning` 3.22 and `chart/critical` 3.77 figures above are ratios on n100, which is `chart/track` in light. `contrast-pairs.json` now checks every ProgressBar, Meter and ProgressRing fill on `chart-track` (`chart-primary`, `icon-success`, `chart-warning`, `chart-critical`, `icon-secondary`). Dark `chart/critical` on the track is 3.10, just above 3:1.
- **Tones Figma has no fill for** use builder-chosen tokens (D-i, pending design): `success` uses `icon/success` (4.06 light, 5.87 dark on the track); `neutral` uses `icon/secondary` (3.75 light, 5.32 dark), because `chart/neutral` is 1.36:1 light and 1.41:1 dark on the track and fails 1.4.11.
- **Dark `icon/critical` is a pairing override, not a contrast fix**: red/500 is 3.11:1 at worst, which passes 3:1. It ships with kind `pairing` so the redundancy rule does not delete it ([§15.4](#154-ownership-and-the-override-mechanism)).
- **Select's trigger hover now reads `border/control-hover`.** Week 1's Select CSS fell back to `border/strong` (1.56:1 on white) on hover; week 2 changed it to `--nz-border-control-hover` (3.75:1 light, 5.32:1 dark), as this remapping says.
- The matrix also checks Alert's text, icons and focus outline on the four `bg/*-subtle` tints, and `text/primary` on `bg/pressed`. In all, 58 checked pairs (29 per theme) pass.

### 15.4 Ownership and the override mechanism

- **Figma remains the source of truth for values.** When it conflicts with AA, code ships a **time-boxed override** of the existing variable in the token source (`src/code-owned/a11y/{light,dark}.overrides.tokens.json`), applied as the last resolver source of each context. It is not a CSS layer, so the CSS, JS refs, Tailwind output and Figma-importable JSON all agree, and the layer order is untouched. A variable or primitive Figma does not have yet is a **proposal**: a semantic colour or motion value in `src/code-owned/proposals/{color,motion}/<mode>.tokens.json` (one file per mode), a primitive or text style in `src/code-owned/proposals.tokens.json` ([§7.6](#76-code-owned-tokens)).
- Each override records `figmaVariableId` (the stable id from `$extensions["dev.nizivo.figma"]`, so the Grove → Nizivo rename cannot orphan it), `figmaVariable` (the display name, for people), `figmaValueAtOverride`, `kind`, `reason`, `wcag`, `issue`, `approvedBy` and `expires`. Each proposal records `figmaVariable` (the prefix-free name to create, for example `text/on-critical`), `wcag` where relevant, `approvedBy` and `expires`.
- **`kind`** is one of three (week 2; D-k): `contrast` (the default: a fix for a failing pair); `ladder` (re-spacing a ladder, not a fix: the two Option A `text/secondary` entries, and the dark surface and border ladder `bg/subtle`, `border/subtle`, `border/default` and `border/strong`); and `pairing` (keeps one token on the same value as its partner although Figma's value passes: dark `icon/critical`, which follows `text/critical`). Only `contrast` overrides take the passes-on-its-own redundancy test (CI rule 3).
- **`issue`** is required: the tracking issue's `https` URL, or an explicit `null` until one is filed. All 14 week 2 overrides carry `null`; no tracker issues exist yet. The tracker itself is open (2026-10-15): GitHub Issues, or a Linear workspace with an MCP server scoped to this project, which the maintainer offered ("if needed, we can make a linear workspace and add mcp scoped to this project"). The check accepts any `https` URL, so either needs no code change.
- **`approvedBy`** is `["maintainer"]` on every entry until a second owner and a CODEOWNERS file exist; the entries ship on the decision 8e and 8f defaults. The two-owner rule below applies from then on. **2026-10-15 (M15):** the second owner is named: Dayitava, the design contact, "can handle both" roles, design-system lead and accessibility owner, for overrides, proposals and the decision meeting. Dayitava's GitHub username is still pending; it goes into CODEOWNERS, and Dayitava's approval goes into `approvedBy` (next to `maintainer`, in the same identifier form CODEOWNERS uses) as Dayitava reviews each entry. Until then the entries keep `["maintainer"]`.

```jsonc
// code-owned/a11y/dark.overrides.tokens.json (one entry)
// token ids as the normalizer writes them: colours sit under `color.` (stripped from CSS names by variableName, §7.3)
{ "color": { "text": { "critical": { "$type": "color", "$value": "{color.p.red.400}",
  "$extensions": { "app.nizivo.override": {
    "figmaVariableId": "VariableID:737:146", "figmaVariable": "Nizivo Color/text/critical (Dark)", "figmaValueAtOverride": "{color.p.red.500}", "kind": "contrast",
    "reason": "4.01:1 on bg/surface, 3.11 on bg/hover (WCAG 1.4.3)", "wcag": ["1.4.3"],
    "issue": null, "approvedBy": ["maintainer"],          // later the issue's https URL (GitHub Issues or Linear: open); both owners
    "expires": "2027-01-31" } } } } } }
```

**CI rules** (`packages/tokens/scripts/a11y.ts` over `build/a11y.ts`, on every PR and every sync PR; while Actions is off, in the local gate). It runs twice: inside the tokens build after `tz build`, and again as the root `pnpm check:a11y` (`--check`) after `pnpm build` in `pnpm check`. The second run is never cached, which rule 4 needs: a cached build replays a pass after the expiry date, because the date is not a cache input.
1. **Contrast matrix:** `contrast-pairs.json` lists every foreground/background pair components use, with its role (text, large text, non-text, focus, decorative, disabled). CI evaluates **every theme permutation** and fails on any AA miss. It does not rely on Terrazzo's per-modifier lint alone, which is unconfirmed.
2. **Drift** (overrides): the Figma value of `figmaVariableId` no longer equals `figmaValueAtOverride` → fail ("Figma changed this token; re-evaluate").
3. **Redundancy:** an override whose Figma value now equals it, or a **contrast** override whose Figma value now passes on its own, or a proposal whose variable Figma now defines (matched by the prefix-free name) → fail ("delete the override"). This is how both files empty themselves. The two Option A `text/secondary` entries are not contrast fixes: Figma's n600/n400 already pass (5.65/5.32), and the entries only re-space the ladder so tertiary can move up. They carry `"kind": "ladder"`, skip the passes-on-its-own test, and leave when Figma adopts Option A (equality) or when design rejects it (expiry). The dark surface and border overrides are `ladder` too, and dark `icon/critical` is `pairing`; neither kind takes the test.
4. **Expiry:** warn 14 days before `expires`, fail after; renewal needs both owners (Dayitava holds both roles, M15; the maintainer alone until Dayitava's GitHub username is in CODEOWNERS). Only an uncached run can see the date change: `pnpm check:a11y`.
5. **Record:** `packages/tokens/CONTRAST.md` lists every live override and proposal. The public docs page that also listed them is off the site under M82; its last state is `docs/figma/deviations.md`.
6. **Release gate:** Changesets refuses a stable publish with any failing pair. An allowlist is permitted only in prereleases.

**RACI:** the design-system designer is **accountable** for values (fixes land in Figma). The engineering accessibility owner is **responsible** for the matrix and may propose overrides. CODEOWNERS on `code-owned/a11y/**` and `code-owned/proposals*` requires both, once a second owner exists; until then the maintainer approves alone, and the repo has no CODEOWNERS file yet. **2026-10-15 (M15):** Dayitava holds both roles for overrides, proposals and the decision meeting, so one review by Dayitava covers both approvals, and the maintainer still reviews and merges every change (decision 7). The CODEOWNERS file waits for Dayitava's GitHub username. The release manager enforces the gate (for 0.1.0, the maintainer running the local release script, M14).

---

## 16. Forms, data tables, dates, i18n and RTL

### 16.1 Forms (gap report A, D16)

- **`Field` is a server-safe shell.** It is sync, calls `useId`, and renders the label and description from props. It wraps its children in a tiny `'use client'` **`FieldScope`** leaf (~0.5 KB), which provides context and renders the error slot. Week 3: FieldScope measures 1.0 KB min+gz on its own (2.7 KB with the anatomy helpers it imports from `field.js`, InlineError and the glyphs; W3-RESULTS.md), because it also owns native validation and renders the whole anatomy (D-q). Week 8: it also renders the announcer, §16.5's one exception (M29); on its own it measures 1,490 B gzip (size-limit, `own: field/field-scope.client`).
- **Zero-JS mode (documented first for Server-Component forms):** pass a function child. `Field` then renders the error itself, and the child receives ready-made `controlProps` (ids, `aria-describedby`, `aria-invalid`, `name`, `required`). Week 3: this is zero execution, not always zero bytes. `field.tsx` imports FieldScope, and Turbopack and `next dev` ship a client module a route's server graph imports even when nothing renders it; production webpack drops it (the Next fixture's `/field-zero-js`). A separate static entry would make it zero bytes everywhere; that is open (§21.1 week 3).
- **Every control is a thin client leaf** (~0.3 KB target each). Its SSR HTML is complete and works before hydration. This overrides r3's "Input is T0 server": a control must read `FieldScope`. Week 3 measured 0.4 to 1.2 KB of own code per leaf (min+gz; Textarea 0.4, Switch 0.6, RadioGroup 0.7, Input 0.8, Checkbox 0.8, SegmentedControl 1.2), within §17.2's 1 KB cap except SegmentedControl; NativeSelect is the exception to the rule and stays `server` (D-x).
- **Group controls** (SegmentedControl; later ChipGroup and third-party pickers) are a root `role="radiogroup"` over several native inputs, not one labelable element. The Field takes `group`: its label renders with no `for`, and the control names its root with `aria-labelledby` (D-s).
- **Native constraint validation stays on.** The `invalid` event is intercepted so messages render inline (React 19.3 delivers it to `onInvalid`; VERIFIED in jsdom). Messages appear **on submit by default** (the GOV.UK pattern); `validateOn="blur"` is opt-in.
- **One error channel**, in priority order: the `error` prop, then `Form errors[name]`, then the native message. Errors use `aria-describedby`, not `aria-errormessage`. `data-invalid` means "a message is presented", never merely "fails constraints".
- **`Form`, `SubmitButton` and `FormMessage`** (v0.2) are optional clients: errors by name, focus on the first invalid control, an optional error summary, and `useFormStatus` → `pending`.
- **Form libraries are docs recipes only:** react-hook-form 7.90, TanStack Form 1.33 and Conform 1.21, with Standard Schema validation ([§10.6](#106-field-and-a-form-library-recipe)). There are no `XField` wrappers and no adapter packages; adapter packages would couple us to RHF 8 beta and TanStack Form 2 alpha.
- **Third-party controls** plug in through `useFieldControl()`. Recipe tests cover all three libraries.
- **Button-based controls** (Select; later the DateRangePicker and Combobox triggers, and third-party button controls) are named through `aria-labelledby`, not through `<label for>`. React Aria builds the trigger's `aria-labelledby` from the value id plus whatever label id it is given, and `aria-labelledby` overrides `<label for>`; without the Field label id the button would be announced only by its value (WCAG 4.1.2, 2.5.3). So the control passes the Field label id (`useFieldControl()` returns it as `labelledBy`). A `<label for>` that points at a `<button>` also turns a label click into a button click, which opens the popup; with `useFieldControl({ labelBehavior: 'focus' })`, `FieldScope` intercepts the label's click, prevents that, and focuses the control instead, as React Aria's own `labelElementType: 'span'` does. The server-safe `Field` shell and the zero-JS function-child path are unchanged. **Built in week 6** for Select (D-aq): `FieldScope` registers the option in an effect, so the server HTML does not change, and focuses the control with `focus({ focusVisible: true })`, so the ring shows. A required, empty Select stops the submit and takes focus but shows no inline message yet (React Aria owns the hidden `<select>`'s `invalid` event); pass `error`.
- **Input family (D20):**

| Item | Tier | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Input (text, email, url, tel) + `prefix`/`suffix`/`leadingIcon` | T0 in a client leaf | Text affixes join `aria-describedby`; icons are `aria-hidden`; the group shows `:focus-within`. Week 3: also `search`, `password` and `number`, `trailingIcon` and sizes; the root `.nz-input` is the box (D-y) |
| Textarea (+ character `count`) | T0 + `field-sizing: content` | Auto-grow with no JS (Baseline Newly 2026-06-16; older browsers fall back to `rows`). The count is `aria-hidden` visually and announced politely only after the user pauses. Soft limit by default. Week 3: `field-sizing` ships inside `@supports (field-sizing: content)`, the one property the Lightning CSS guard allows only there; Firefox 129 and Safari 17.5 keep `rows` (D-w). The count is not built yet |
| PasswordInput | own leaf (~0.4 KB) | `aria-pressed` toggle with a constant label; reverts to `type="password"` on submit |
| SearchField | T0: `<search>` + `type="search"` | Zero-JS filtering with `<form method="get">` |
| QuantityStepper | T0 `type="number"` + own two-button leaf | Integer only; buttons `tabIndex={-1}`; arrows from the native spinbutton |
| NumberField (currency, percent, locale parsing) | T1 (+9.8 KB) | Native `type="number"` mishandles locale separators |
| Combobox | T1 (+9.8 KB) | Never hand-built; `<datalist>` rejected |

### 16.2 Data tables (gap report A, D17)

- **Native `<table>` server-safe parts in core** (`@nizivo/react/table`, 0 KB client JS): container, table, header, body, row, head, cell, sort button. Semantics are `table`, never `grid`.
- **One form-based selection island** plus `BulkActionBar` (≤ 2 KB target). Selection is tri-state on the header checkbox. Rows use `data-selected`, never `aria-selected` inside a `table`.
- **Server-driven sort, filter and pagination through the URL**, using `render={<Link/>}` on sort buttons and Pagination. The page is the state; there is no client data engine in core.
- **`DataView`** (v0.3) renders both Table and List presentations and swaps them with a container query at `sm`. It is the only SSR-deterministic way to do 08's Table ↔ List swap. Built in week 25 (D-db).
- **TanStack Table v9 and TanStack Virtual appear only in registry blocks** (`data-table`, `data-table-virtual`), never as npm dependencies of ours. Their 12.9–17.0 KB stays in the app that chose them.
- **Rejected:** the React Aria `Table` (+29.0 KB over the dashboard set, `role="grid"`).
- **Large data:** pagination first; virtualisation is opt-in in the registry block. `content-visibility: auto` does nothing on `<tr>` (CSS Containment excludes internal table boxes), but it works on the List presentation.
- Sticky header via `--nz-z-sticky` and the `--nz-table-sticky-offset` hook; horizontal scroll inside the container with `data-focus-inset` cells.
- *Week 16 ([D-bz](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14)):* the server parts are built (`@nizivo/react/table`: Container, Root, Caption, Header, Body, Footer, Row, Head, Cell, SortButton, RowLink). Not yet: the selection island, `TableEmpty`, `TableSkeletonRows` and the sticky header (a header cannot stick to the page inside the container that scrolls the table sideways).

### 16.3 Dates and time (gap report A, D18)

- **Public values are ISO plain-date strings** (`'2026-10-14'`), never `Date`. They serialise across the RSC boundary and carry no time-zone ambiguity. Ranges are `{ start, end }`.
- **Internally, `@internationalized/date` 3.12.4** is an explicit dependency of the date entries. It is already a dependency of React Aria Components, so it adds nothing for most apps.
- **No Temporal polyfill:** it costs 19.3 KB, and Safari stable has not shipped Temporal (BCD: `preview`). Because the public type is a string, moving internals to native Temporal later is a **patch**.
- **`timeZone` is required** wherever "today", relative presets or "now" are involved.
- **The trigger is the SSR surface** (≤ 1.5 KB eager); **the calendar is client-only and lazy** (+21.4 KB over the dashboard set → budget 24 KB).
- Ships as **`@nizivo/react/unstable/date-range-picker`** in v0.3, until design draws the day-cell states (today, outside month, unavailable, range start/end/in range).
- The Figma shape (a button trigger + popover + presets list + RangeCalendar, no segmented `DateInput`) measured 70.1 KB standalone. Skipping `DateInput` saves about 7 KB.
- Open design questions (Apply/Cancel vs instant commit, one or two months, compare-to-previous-period) are in [figma-handoff.md](figma-handoff.md). Figma's 746:267 (read 2026-10-27) answers two: Apply/Cancel, one month. Built in week 27 (D-dg), eager for now (M71).

### 16.4 Internationalisation and RTL (gap report B, Part A)

- **Strings.** About 60 built-in strings exist (for example "Close", "Loading {label}", "Showing {from}–{to} of {total}", "{count} rows selected").
  - Each part takes a serialisable **`labels` prop** with English defaults. The optional client `NizivoConfig` can supply `labels` globally: **not built in 0.1.0** (`NizivoConfigProps` has only `locale`), so `labels` is per instance and an app wraps a part once to pass its own. Server-safe parts (Button) could not read a context anyway. Button's `labels.pending` ("Loading…", M34) is the first default that is visible text rather than an accessible name, which raises the priority of a global route (week 9 review A11Y-3).
  - Plain-object catalogues ship at `@nizivo/react/locales/<tag>` (v0.2), starting with `en-US` only. Others are added on request.
  - Plurals use CLDR category objects (`{ one, other }`) through a ~0.2 KB `formatLabel` built on `Intl.PluralRules`. There is no MessageFormat dependency (FormatJS measured 9.65 KB). Strings are never concatenated.
  - A catalogue lint checks plural categories per locale.
  - **Engine-rendered strings.** React Aria-backed parts render some strings from React Aria's own 36-locale catalogues (for example NumberField "Increase", DatePicker segment labels and Toast region labels; gap report B §A1). Nizivo's `labels` do not control them, an app i18n stack cannot translate them for other locales, and an engine swap would silently change them (Base UI ships no strings). So each `<name>.contract.ts` lists them as `<Name>Labels` keys; adapters pass them into the engine wherever it accepts overrides, and the rest are listed as "engine-owned" on the component's docs page. A conformance case with a locale outside React Aria's 36 checks that every accessible name comes from Nizivo labels or that list. An engine swap re-maps them ([§4.1](#41-layers) L4).
- **Locale determinism.** The default locale is `'en-US'`; nothing reads `navigator.language` during render. Every React Aria root is wrapped in `I18nProvider` with the resolved locale. A conformance test sets the browser to `de-DE` and asserts no text changes after hydration.
- **Direction.** `NizivoConfig` has **no `direction` key**. React Aria derives direction from the locale; our own code reads it from the DOM when an event fires; CSS uses logical properties and `:dir()` only. **Tabs (M26, week 7; A11Y-4):** the arrow keys follow the `NizivoConfig` scope's locale direction, and `Tabs.Root` takes `dir?: 'ltr' | 'rtl'` (rendered as its `dir` attribute) to override it for that strip; a `Tabs.Root` without `dir` takes the nearest enclosing `Tabs.Root`'s `dir`, so a strip nested in a `dir` root's panel keys the way the inherited attribute lays it out (week 7 review). React Aria's only direction input is the locale `I18nProvider` gets, so the tab list alone gets a carrier locale with that direction: the scope's own when it already matches (every render without `dir` is unchanged), else `ar` or `en`. Nothing in the list formats or speaks a string, each tab's content gets the scope locale back (a consumer's formatter in a label stays in the scope's language; week 7 review), and the panels keep the scope locale. **Do not retry a script subtag as the carrier:** WebKit 27.2 reads `Intl.Locale` text direction from the language alone (`en-Arab-US`, `de-Arab-DE` and `und-Arab` read `ltr`; Chromium 156, Firefox 157 and Node 24 read `rtl`), and a language swap over whole engine subtrees would change React Aria's own strings in Select, Menu and Dialog, which is why the scope keeps no direction key. Nothing reads the DOM, so `dir="auto"` is not accepted. `stylelint-use-logical` runs from the first commit (cheap now, expensive later). `dirOf(locale)` helps apps set `<html dir>`.
- **Formatting.** `Intl` with an explicit `locale` and `timeZone`; `format` takes a serialisable `Intl.*Options` object or a named preset; values are formatted once where they first render; formatted numbers are wrapped in `unicode-bidi: isolate` and use tabular figures.
- **Icons.** The curated `mirror.json` subset auto-mirrors under RTL, with a per-instance opt-out. Media, clocks, checks, search, rings and trend icons never mirror.
- **Text expansion.** Tab-bar labels truncate with an ellipsis plus `aria-label` (08 has 75 px tabs).
- **Testing.** A 10-locale matrix plus two pseudo-locales. ar-EG enters visual regression at v1.0 (he-IL for spot checks). A mirror-geometry Playwright test lands in v0.3.
- **React Aria locale strings** (36 locales) add 4.8 KB to the dashboard set unless apps use `@react-aria/optimize-locales-plugin`. Budgets are measured **with** all locales (the worst case), and docs give the plugin recipe per framework.

### 16.5 Loading, empty and error contract

From gap report B, Part C. It applies to every data-display component (KpiTile, Metric, TrendCard, charts, Table, DataView, RankedList):
- **API:** `status: 'ready' | 'loading' | 'empty' | 'error'`, plus `pending` (a refresh is in flight while showing data) and `empty`/`error` element slots. `status` is reserved for this lifecycle; domain states use domain names (`availability`, `paymentStatus`, `fulfillmentStatus`).
- **The Suspense fallback is the same component with `status="loading"`.** The frame persists and only the content region swaps. A test asserts the root box is identical (±1 px) across all four states at three container widths, so streaming causes no layout shift.
- **The library never fetches and never suspends.**
- **Attributes:** `data-loading`, `data-pending`, `data-empty`, `data-error`. `data-error` (a load failure) is distinct from `data-invalid` (validation).
- **Accessibility:** `aria-busy` plus static visually hidden text ("Loading Revenue"); **no live region inside skeletons**. `InlineError` takes an `action` slot. **The one exception (M29, 2026-10-17; built in week 8): FieldScope's announcer.** A failed submit moves focus to the form's first invalid control, which reads its error through `aria-describedby`; when focus is already in that control, nothing moves and nothing would be read. So each FieldScope renders one visually hidden polite live region (`aria-live="polite"`, `aria-atomic="true"`), the last child of the Field root, empty in the server HTML so it exists before any message. It is armed by a validation request made while focus is in the Field (an `invalid` event at the control when it is the form's first invalid control, or a form `submit` heard on the document in the capture phase), and it speaks the next error text that commits while focus is still inside, once: the native message even when unchanged, an `error` prop only when its text changes (a commit that empties the error keeps the request, so an app that clears its errors when a submit starts has the reply spoken). It says nothing at first render and hydration (Strict Mode included), when the request moves focus, at a control that is not the form's first invalid one (focus goes there), while the user types (an input event disarms it), for a reply whose text equals the error shown, or to repeat the text it holds; it reads the message, not the mark, and empties after 7 s. The zero-JS path has none, and every other part, InlineError and the Field's error included, stays not live. Rules: `packages/react/src/field/FIELD-CONTRACT.md` "The announcer"; tests: `test/browser/field-announcer.spec.tsx` and `test/ssr/field.test.tsx`. Real screen-reader output is part of the manual pass (exit criterion 14, NIZ-78).
- **Pending UI:** Button `loading` is renamed **`pending`**; it stays focusable with `aria-disabled`. Every stateful part is controllable, so `useOptimistic` can drive it; optimistic rows use `data-pending`; controls are never disabled during a transition.
- Client islands fail closed: if an island throws, the server-rendered content stays.
- **Built in week 12 (M41)** for Metric, KpiTile, TrendCard and RankedList: one shared helper, `packages/react/src/_internal/data-status.ts` (`DataStatus`, `dataStatusAttrs(status, pending)`), writes the attributes and `aria-busy`; a ready part with no value renders as empty; loading shows a skeleton and static visually hidden text ("Loading Total sales"), never a live region. Browser specs assert the root box identical (±1 px) across the four statuses in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, each status alone in a cell of a `repeat(4, 1fr)` row and at 100 % and 200 % text since the week 12 review (A11Y-W12-2; the first test shared one column between statuses and could not see a width change): KpiTile (also a bare fallback and an error with a Retry) and Metric at 200/240/320/480 px, TrendCard at 320/560/800 (also a bare fallback), RankedList at 280/380/560 and with labels longer than the row. To hold it, KpiTile, TrendCard and RankedList take no minimum width from their content (`min-inline-size: 0`), TrendCard fills its container and, under a 30rem header (a size container), puts its action below the headline and the change below the number, both reserved in every status (A11Y-W12-3), and a RankedList label is one line with an ellipsis (A11Y-W12-4); [D-bj](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14). The Next fixture's `/dashboard` streams the KPI row and RankedList behind Suspense with their loading parts as fallbacks; the load's layout shifts sum to 0 in every mode (a fallback of another size measured 0.0557 in the negative control).

## 17. Performance budgets (measured)

**Method** (settled 13). Sizes are min + **gzip**, React and ReactDOM external, using r3's esbuild method (bundle + minify, ESM, production condition, gzip -9) with **all 36 React Aria locales included**, the worst case a consumer can hit. Gap report A and verdict V8 reproduced r3's dashboard baseline exactly (69.0 KB). Each limit is the measurement + ~10%. Rows marked "target" are UNVERIFIED until the first build, then become hard `size-limit` entries. `size-limit` 14.2 is configured for gzip so the numbers stay comparable (r4's brotli ceilings are not used; critique C23).

**Week 5: the gate and Nizivo's own numbers** (D-ah; `packages/react/SIZES.md`, `packages/react/W5-RESULTS.md`). `pnpm size` runs 118 size-limit checks inside `pnpm check`, with budgets at Nizivo's measured + 10% in `packages/tooling/size/budgets.json`, **never above the limits in the table above** (`PLAN_LIMITS_KIB` in `packages/tooling/size/method.ts`; the week 5 review found the first budgets sat 2 to 7 KiB above them). The measured rows above are r3's measurements of **React Aria alone**; Nizivo's subpaths add their own layer and are all under the limits today, so the gate enforces the limits as written: Tooltip 16,327 B (gated at 16 KiB, 57 B headroom), Dialog 31,445 B (31, 299 B), Tabs 33,577 B (35), Menu 56,623 B (57), Select 62,404 B (66), the dashboard set 76,153 B = 74.4 KiB (75, read as KiB). Growing past one is a labelled change to this table first (§17.3). The React Aria part of each subpath is at or under r3 (Select 57.5 KiB, Dialog 28.1, Tooltip 13.7, Menu 50.7, Tabs 31.1). Seam cost: Nizivo costs +1.6 KB (Tabs, Tooltip) to +4.5 KB (Menu) gzip over a hand-written React Aria layer with the same parts, +6.5 KB for all five together (76.2 vs 69.7 KB raw). **Open for the maintainer, reported not decided:** whether the dashboard set's "75" is KiB (74.4 passes) or decimal kB (76.2 fails) (settled by M17: KiB, and the limit is now 76); K6 at 757 B against its ≤ 0.5 KB estimate; Field at 3,136 B against §17.2's 2.5 KB target. The `budget` fields `component.json` carried (r3's figures, read by nothing) were removed after the review.

**Week 12 (M41):** no engine-family limit moved and no §17.1 limit was raised. The dashboard set measures 77,362 B (76 KiB), unchanged; the root barrel `@nizivo/react` grows 86,521 → 91,326 B with the nine new parts (limit 93 KiB, not raised). `pnpm size` runs 149 checks (week 11: 130). Only DonutChart's and the island's raises are M41's (item 2); the `styles.css` limit and the new entries above their §17.2 targets (CountUp's own code, seven of the eight server subpaths) were provisional, set by the integrator (week 12 review R2-1, R2-2, R2-3, R3-2). **M42 (2026-10-20) settled all three:** `@nizivo/react/styles.css` keeps **19 KiB as a labelled raise by M42** item 1 (from 16; 17,232 B after M42); CountUp's 1.2 KiB own code and 1.7 KiB `./motion` are settled budgets (item 2); the ≤ 1 KB SPA target for server parts is restated per leaf part (item 3, §17.2).

**Escape-hatch reference (not the default):** React Aria hooks with our own DOM measured Menu 39.6 KB and Menu + Select + Tabs 45.3 KB, vs 51.3 and 65.5 for RAC components (verdict V8, reproduced twice). If a component moves to the hooks altitude, every lazy chunk is **re-measured on that base**: verdict V9 found RangeCalendar is +25.1 KB over the hooks base, not +21.4, and an RAC Autocomplete-based CommandMenu is +19.9 KB, not +4.7.

### 17.3 Page-level arithmetic and runtime performance

- **A server-only page** (cards, KPI tiles, tables, buttons, charts without interaction): 0 KB of Nizivo client JS.
- **The overview dashboard (08) in v0.2:** the dashboard set (≤ 75 KB, shared core) + table selection (≤ 2) + the charts island's client graph (≤ 1.7, once per page however many charts use it; M40, M41 item 2) + Toast increment (measured in v0.2). Lazy surfaces (calendar, command menu) load on first use.
- **Runtime:** Vitest 5 `bench` in browser mode with a Profiler; a regression above 15% fails. The v0.2 exit requires CLS < 0.01 on a streamed overview page with delayed Suspense boundaries. Web Vitals for the RSC + React Aria hydration cost are recorded on the docs site and fixtures from v0.2 (critique G22).
- **Budgets only go up through a labelled PR.** The PR comment shows the `size-limit` diff, produced by a small custom step (the community `size-limit-action` has not been released since April 2024). While Actions is off there is no PR comment: `node packages/tooling/size/measure.ts` prints measured against recorded locally, `--write` records measurements without raising a limit, and `--rebase` (a labelled change) resets limits to measured + 10%.

## 18. Quality: testing, visual regression, a11y automation, docs, Storybook

### 18.1 Test layers

| Layer | Tool | Runs | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unit and interaction | Vitest 5.0.5 Browser Mode + `@vitest/browser-playwright` + `vitest-browser-react`, `getByRole` locators | Chromium on PRs; Firefox and WebKit on `main` and nightly | Real CSS, focus-visible, dialogs and layout need a real browser. Any console warning or error fails the test. **W6:** the browser lane and the overlay suite run in Chromium and Playwright's Firefox in `pnpm check:full`, the overlay suite in WebKit too (D-ar); the browser lane's solid-outline check covers every T0 control |
| SSR | Node-environment suite: `renderToString` of every example; every entry loads under the default condition; server-safe entries load under `react-server` | every PR | Catches a hook or browser API in a server-safe module. W2: `test/ssr/render-all.test.tsx` renders all 27 examples and checks every emitted `data-*` against the vocabulary; `test/ssr/t0.server.test.tsx` loads all 19 T0 entries and renders their examples with `react` mocked to React's react-server build (a stub dispatcher serves `useId`), not through a real resolve condition. The Next fixture is the real RSC render |
| Conformance | `@nizivo/testkit` per component per engine, plus the O-tests ([§9.6](#96-conformance-suite), [§12.5](#125-overlay-conformance-tests)) | PRs (Chromium), `main` (all) | Asserts classes, `data-slot` and `data-*`, not only behaviour |
| Framework fixtures | `fixtures/{next,vite,astro,…}` install the **packed** packages and run one shared Playwright spec from `fixtures/runner` (`@nizivo/fixture-runner`). How (W1, `fixtures/next/RESULTS.md`): the runner unpacks each `pnpm pack` tarball and the fixture installs the folders as `file:` dependencies, which pnpm copies into its store (never a workspace link) with no integrity hash, so a committed lockfile stays valid across builds and installs with `--frozen-lockfile`. A tarball itself cannot carry a stable lockfile, because its hash changes with every build. Each fixture is its own pnpm workspace root, excluded from the monorepo (`'!fixtures/next'`), because the packed `@nizivo/react` depends on an unpublished exact `@nizivo/tokens` and only a workspace root can override that | every PR (path-filtered), nightly in full. **While Actions is off (W5, D-ak):** `pnpm fixture:next`, `fixture:vite` and `fixture:astro`, all three in `pnpm check:full` and the release script | Zero console errors, zero recoverable hydration errors, interactivity after hydration, axe, no theme flash, no `@nizivo` client chunk on server-only pages. **W5:** Next (23 pages, four modes), Vite (12) and Astro (12) all pass, with axe-core 4.14.0 injected as source (WCAG 2.0–2.2 A/AA, light) on every page and a theme-flash probe that records every frame from first paint (`fixtures/runner/theme.ts`; its negative control, a theme set late, is caught) |
| Accessibility | axe-core **4.14.0**, a direct catalog pin. W2 docs step: no pnpm override is needed while `@axe-core/playwright` (which pins axe-core ~4.13) is not installed, because the docs smoke test injects axe-core's own source into Playwright pages; the override comes back with the Storybook addon or `@axe-core/playwright`. Storybook a11y addon with `test: 'error'` and `wcag2a/2aa/21a/21aa/22aa`; `@axe-core/playwright` on docs and fixtures in light, dark, forced colours and reduced motion; oxlint `jsx-a11y` rules | every PR | Dark results are reported, not blocking, while dark is `preview` (*blocking since M62, 2026-10-25*) |
| Screen readers | Guidepup (`@guidepup/playwright` 0.19.1): NVDA on `windows-2025`, VoiceOver on macOS | nightly from v1.0; non-blocking until green for 30 days | Select, Combobox, Menu, Tabs, Dialog, Toast, form groups, charts. Manual AT pass every minor until then |
| Visual regression | Vitest `toMatchScreenshot` in a pinned Playwright Docker image; baselines committed as ordinary files (Git LFS only if they outgrow plain Git). No hosted service (decision 6, zero spend); stories stay CSF3, so a vendor could be added later. **Built in W5 as a plain Node Playwright runner instead (D-ai):** `packages/react/scripts/visual/run.ts` over a harness of every example, 1,496 committed baselines (4.7 MB), its own lossless PNG encoder and a compare exact to ±2 per channel (3 stray pixels per shot allowed; pixelmatch's YIQ and anti-aliasing tests are opt-in); `baselines/manifest.json` pins the environment (darwin-arm64, Chromium 156.0.8078.4) and the run refuses elsewhere, until Linux baselines are recorded in a pinned image | sync PRs and `main`. **While Actions is off:** `pnpm test:visual` (about 45 s), in `pnpm check:full` and the release script, not in `pnpm check` (D-ak) | v0.1 matrix: light, desktop. **W5 covers light, dark, forced colours and forced colours with a dark OS** (rest, hover, pressed, focus by keyboard, open) on desktop. Mobile and RTL by v1.0. Full matrices only on the merge queue |

| Package correctness | publint 0.3.25 and attw 0.18.5 (profile esm-only, CSS entries excluded) inside the build; Node 22/24 resolution test; exports drift; directive guard; tarball allowlist | every PR | On packed tarballs. W2: the root `pnpm check:release` (`packages/tooling/release/check.ts`) packs `@nizivo/tokens` and `@nizivo/react` and runs the tarball allowlist with published-`package.json` rules, publint (strict), attw and the directive guard with the `export * as` lint on each, as the last step of `pnpm check`. The allowlist names exact files (`LICENSE`, `README.md`, `package.json`, and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` for react), not §19.3's globs. It also packs `@nizivo/icons` (since W4). **W5:** all three packages ship a README (install, the `next` dist-tag, the CSS import rule, the support floor), and the allowlist now requires a non-empty `README.md` in each tarball; `pnpm pack` does not include `CHANGELOG.md` |
| API surface | `contracts/api/**` regenerated and diffed; the changeset must classify the change. W2 docs step: `nz api` writes 51 files (27 React entries, 24 stylesheets) from the built `@nizivo/react` and `@nizivo/tokens` (D-o), and the root `pnpm gen:check` builds `@nizivo/react` and runs `nz api --check`. Classifying the change in the changeset is not automated yet | every PR | The props table and hooks table in the docs come from the same files |
| Performance | `size-limit` (gzip), Vitest `bench` | every PR. **W5:** `pnpm size` (118 checks, about 1 s; 119 with `styles.css` in W6) runs inside `pnpm check` | [§17](#17-performance-budgets-measured); budgets and method D-ah |

**Cache inputs (2026-10-20, M39).** Turbo replays a task's cached result when the task's hash is unchanged, so a file the task reads but turbo does not hash is a missing input: change only that file and the replay is a stale pass. `pnpm cache:audit` (`packages/tooling/cache-audit/`) runs every turbo task of `pnpm check` once, from a copy of the repo that holds only the files turbo hashes for it (its inputs, the global inputs, and its dependencies' inputs and outputs), with a `node:fs` trace preloaded into every Node process. A task fails the audit when it fails in the copy (which also catches native readers: tsgo, oxlint, Rolldown, Turbopack), when its outputs differ from the repo's (the docs build and typecheck are exempt: Next's build ids and chunk names change between runs), or when it read a repo file turbo does not hash for it. Its first run found five: the root `package.json` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (vitest, Terrazzo and Next read them to find the workspace root), `RELEASING.md` (the tooling tests), the built react and tokens packages (the tooling tests checked `contracts/api` only when they existed), the stylelint contract's part lists across react and charts, and react's `scripts/css` (charts' typecheck); `turbo.json` declares them all now. A series of commits is gated with each commit's `pnpm check` from the shared cache followed by `pnpm cache:audit`, so a commit whose audit passes cannot pass on a replay, and the last commit of the series runs `pnpm check` with `TURBO_FORCE=true` as well; the release script always runs it forced. Since M64 the gate runs that last commit first, so the earlier commits audit only the tasks whose inputs differ from it (`--in-order` keeps the old order), and the docs build hashes `apps/docs/lib/plan.json`, the plan's numbered headings, §18.2 and §23.4 copied by `nz gen`, instead of the whole plan. The docs smoke is the cached task `@nizivo/docs#smoke` (`pnpm docs:check`, after `@nizivo/docs#build`); it serves on a free port and loads six pages at a time (`--pages`), about 50 s instead of 135 s. Week 11 (M40) added the reads of `nz api` for charts and of the charts docs routes: `@nizivo/tooling#test` depends on `@nizivo/charts#build` and hashes `packages/charts/src/**`, and `@nizivo/docs#typecheck` hashes the charts `package.json`, barrel and examples; the audit passes all 24 tasks.

### 18.2 Docs site (public)

- **Fumadocs 16.17.2 on Next 16.4** at nizivo.neevtechnologies.in. It is also the largest RSC consumer of the library.
- **Hosting (zero spend):** since M67 (2026-10-27) a static export (`output: 'export'`) deployed to **Cloudflare Pages** by a command the maintainer runs locally, so no Actions run is needed; before that the plan was GitHub Pages from the public repo, with the custom domain through a DNS `CNAME` record at neevtechnologies.in, which the maintainer adds later, before the hosting step (2026-10-15). A static export keeps every free static host available, Cloudflare Pages included. Vercel's free Hobby plan is for non-commercial personal use, so it is not used for a company project. Search is the built-in static index, so no server is needed. While GitHub Actions is off ([§1](#1-decisions-we-need-from-you)), the docs build is local only: no Pages configuration and no workflow file. The Fumadocs skeleton was built in the separate step right after week 2 (**Docs skeleton status**, [§21.1](#211-v01--foundations-forms-and-core-overlays--6-weeks--2-weeks-buffer-dist-tag-next); `apps/docs/RESULTS.md`).
- Live examples come from the `*.example.tsx` modules, rendered by a server `<Example>` that also shows their Shiki-highlighted source. W2 docs step (D-n): `apps/docs/scripts/generate.ts` copies each module into `apps/docs/.generated/examples/` with its relative imports rewritten to the public subpaths, and writes one route per component that imports only its own examples. One route for every component would put every example's client modules on every page: Next bundles every client reference a route's server graph imports, rendered or not, and dynamic `import()` does not change that. Props tables come from `contracts/api/react/*.json`, which `nz api` writes from the built `dist/*.d.ts` with ts-morph 28 (which bundles TS 6.0.2; D-o). Hook and token tables come from `contracts/api/css` and `contract.json`. A hook's description is published only from a `/** … */` doc comment in the source CSS; plain `/* … */` comments are maintainer notes and never reach the site. The **"Deviations from Figma"** page (from the docs skeleton step to 2026-10-28) listed the overrides, the proposals, the builder-chosen values, every [§23.4](#234-builder-defaults-2026-10-14) builder default and these degradations. Under M82 it is off the public site and kept as a record, `docs/figma/deviations.md`; `packages/tokens/CONTRAST.md` holds the live override and proposal tables. The browser degradations the floor lane records (exit criterion 6; `fixtures/floor-lane/RESULTS.md`; D3 and D4 joined in week 4, when Mobile Safari 17.5 ran on the iOS 17.5 Simulator):
  - **D1.** "Firefox 129–131: `light-dark()` with literal colours written directly on a non-inherited property can take the wrong scheme in nested scopes (Firefox bug 1914735, fixed in 132). Nizivo's tokens are unaffected; write theme colours as custom properties."
  - **D2.** "Firefox (all versions tested, 129 to 157): an exit transition that ends in `display: none` is instant, because Firefox cannot animate `display` (bug 1834877). Entry animations work. Engine-managed overlays are unaffected; a CSS-only component that needs an exit animation hides with `visibility` instead."
  - **D3.** "Firefox 129–157, WebKit 27.2 and Mobile Safari 17.5 have no `overlay` property, so a native popover or `<dialog>` exit is instant there. Nizivo's overlays are engine-managed and stay mounted through `data-exiting`, so they are unaffected; this is why no overlay swaps to the native element yet (§12.4 gate 1)."
  - **D4.** "Mobile Safari 17.5 (iOS 17.5 Simulator): an exit transition that ends in `display: none` is instant, as in Firefox (D2): `transition-behavior: allow-discrete` parses, but `display` does not transition. Entry animations work, and WebKit 27.2 fades. Engine-managed overlays are unaffected; a CSS-only component that needs an exit animation hides with `visibility` instead."
- Every component page opens with its examples (M80), then Import and the notes, then the reference: Props or Parts, slots, CSS hooks and Accessibility (keyboard map, APG pattern). The Figma section went under M76; node ids stay in `component-map.json`.
- Built-in static search and `llms.txt` (both built in the docs skeleton step; the search index loads only when a reader first opens search). A lazy "Open in StackBlitz" (`@stackblitz/sdk`) is deferred: it calls an external service, and the site makes no external runtime call.
- Getting-started pages for Next, Vite and Astro (v0.1), then React Router (v0.2) and TanStack Start (v0.3), each including the Tailwind recipe and the CSS import rule. Since W5 the Next, Vite and Astro pages are checked against their fixtures (no draft notes remain); the Vite page carries the `build.cssTarget` step.
- **v0.2:** commerce, storefront and template examples (from `patterns/**`). **v0.3:** the same sources build the registry at `/r/v{x.y.z}/`.

### 18.3 Storybook (internal workshop)

- **Storybook 10.6.2** with `@storybook/react-vite` (not `@storybook/nextjs`, which Storybook 11 deprecates), addon-vitest, addon-a11y, addon-themes and storybook-addon-pseudo-states.
- Stories are plain **CSF3** wrapping the same `*.example.tsx` modules plus edge cases. Docs never depend on Storybook and Storybook never depends on the docs, so either can be replaced alone (Ladle reads CSF3 as a fallback).
